2.2.5 CONCLUSIONS 



The mos significan resul of he SERV base flow es was ha he engine 
.exhaus je and base flow field were ypically charac eris ic of aerospike 
engines. The in egra ed SERV concep ,wi h large area ra io a open wake and 
low engine pressure ra ios, did no indica e any new or differen rends from 
previous reference es da a. Engine performance did prove o be sensi ive o 
forebody geome ry slips ream and he exhaus plume expanding in o he base area. 
This of course was due o he large por ion of engine hrus which is de ermined 
by hese areas for SERV. The necessary da a were acquired, however, o assess 
 he influence of all hese parame ers for he specific SERV vehicle applica ion, 
wi hin he accuracy level of a Phase A feasibili y s udy. The basis for ex ra­
pola ion o full-scale fligh performance was es ablished and he comparison 
wi h original predic ions was good '(see appendix A for de ailed analysis and 
discussion). ·

3. 5 CONCLUSIONS 



Conclusions were summarized in he·following paragraphs. 



Experimen ally acquired da a were generally in agreemen wi h predic ed rends. 



A Mach numbers below 1.2, payload.varia ion did no appreciably af.foc normal 
and axial force charac eris ics •. A angles of a ack grea er han 2 degrees, 
 he effec on pi ching momen was qui e significan . 



A higher Mach numbers, payload shape has a significan effec on all aerody~ 
.·· namic charac eris ics. The longer, larger-diame er payload shapes produce he 
highes normal forces and pi chin@: momen s. bu produce minimum axial forces. 
The primary effec of a change in he opening of he aerospike engine doors 
from 25 degrees o 45 degrees is abou a 25 percen increase in he engine-off 
base drag. 



All payload shapes exhibi marginal s a ic s abili y charac eris ics wi h 
cen er of pressure loca ions in he order of 0.2 o 0.4 calibers forward of 
 he base.

AEROSPIKE PERFORMANCE CORRELATIONS 



A large body of da a has been genera ed for he aerospike nozzle under

been used o develop a me hod of calcula ion· of overall aerospike nozzle 



performance, This me hod predic s he primary nozzle con ribu ion and

Successful heore ical and empirical,me hods of base pressure pred c ion

of pressure ra ios including design pressure ra io, _In his range,

affec ing base pressure, The problem of predic ion of base.pressure 



here is herefore grea ly simplified, In he range of pressure ra ios 



which includes sea level, he base pressure is affec ed by he ambien 



pressure and i s heore ical and empirical predic ion is more complex, 



Because of his, overall nozzle performance predic ion is made in wo 



dis inc ways, one for he closed-wake and one for he open-wake.

s udies have been conduc ed o provide a 10:1 hro ling capabili y in he

A redu_c ion in urbine flow requiremen s for he engine was also indica ed. 



The reduc ion in secondary flow resul s in par of he increased engine I. 
s

0.0264. To provide hro ling of he engine o 10 percen of i s nominal 



 hrus ., he oxidizer injec or pressure drop had o be increased o 2000 



psia o insure proper combus ion in he chamber.

psia. This addi ional requiremen on he urbomachinery was me by increas­



ing he urbine flow ra io ( b" ) o 0,0295.
